Hmm, the answer: This is basically known. The point: the SC symplectic divisor definition includes existence of a "regularization" — normal crossings symplectic divisors are defined as SC divisors that admit compatible structure. The key theorem: every SC symplectic divisor is isotopic through SC symplectic divisors inside (M,ω) to one admitting an ω-regularization; moreover, given an ω-regularization, there exists an ω-tame almost complex structure J compatible with it. The compatibility condition (J∘dΨ = dΨ∘J_{R;I}) ensures that near the stratum, J looks like a product complex structure. Then one can find local coordinates near D in which the "divisorial" coordinates z_1,...,z_r are J-holomorphic and D = z_1⋯z_r = 0.

Let me recall the definitions.

Definition (SC symplectic divisor): finite transverse union V = ∪ V_i of closed codim-2 symplectic submanifolds, with ω-orientation agreement on intersections.

Definition (normal crossings (NC) symplectic divisor): an SC symplectic divisor together with a normal crossings structure / normalization satisfying certain conditions (intersections of intersections are symplectic, etc.), and ultimately existence of a "regularization".

Key theorem (Li–Wu? / Tehrani–Zinger?): The space Symp^+(X, V) is weakly homotopy equivalent to Aux(X,V) (space of pairs (ω, regularization)) and to AK(X,V) (space of (ω, R, J)). In particular, every ω ∈ Symp^+(X,V) can be connected by a path of cohomologous forms to one admitting a regularization and then a compatible tame J.

Hmm wait — actually the Theorem 7 statement says: for each ω ∈ Symp^+(X,V), there's a path (ω_t) of cohomologous symplectic forms in Symp^+(X,V) with ω_0 = ω and V admits an ω_1-regularization. Also, there exists J compatible (i.e., AK(X,V) nonempty over ω_1). Also "Theorem 7 implies that every SC symplectic divisor is isotopic to one admitting a nice compatible almost complex structure."

So the strategy: 

1. Start with (M, ω), D symplectic SC divisor.
2. By the h-principle/weak homotopy equivalence theorem (arXiv:1410.0609 Theorem 2.12, or Tehrani–Zinger "Singularities and semistable degenerations for symplectic topology"), replace ω by a cohomologous ω' connected via path in Symp^+(M,D) such that D admits an ω'-regularization R.
3. Given (ω', R), the space AK(M,D) is nonempty: there exists an ω'-tame almost complex structure J compatible with R (i.e., R-compatible: J preserves TD_i, and intertwines with J_{R;I} on normal bundles).
4. Given a compatible J, construct local coordinates near each point p ∈ D in which z_1,...,z_r are J-holomorphic and D = {z_1⋯z_r = 0}.

Step 4 is the crucial geometric construction: compatibility with regularization implies that near the stratum D_I = ∩_{i∈I} D_i, the divisor coordinates can be made J-holomorphic. How?

Given the regularization data: for each i, there's a neighborhood Dom(Ψ_i) ⊂ NX D_i and a diffeomorphism Ψ_i: NX D_i ⊇ Dom(Ψ_i) → M with Ψ_i|_{D_i} = id (identifying normal bundle with tubular neighborhood). For intersections, Ψ_I = composition, with compatibility. The complex structure on NX D_I induced by J|_{D_I} and fiberwise complex structure i on fibers. The compatibility J∘dΨ_I = dΨ_I ∘ J_{R;I} means: in the "coordinates" (x, v) ∈ NX D_I ≅ D_I ⊕ NX D_I (via connection), J in these coordinates is the product: J(x, v) = (J|_{D_I} x-part, i v). Setup and definitions:

Definition (SC symplectic divisor). Let (M^{2n}, ω) be symplectic (closed, but closedness isn't crucial). A simple crossings (SC) symplectic divisor is a finite union D = ∪_{i∈S} D_i of closed codimension-2 submanifolds such that {D_i} is a transverse collection (any subcollection intersects transversely, possibly empty intersection), each stratum D_I = ∩_{i∈I} D_i is a symplectic submanifold (for nonempty I), and the intersection orientation agrees with the ω-orientation on each stratum of positive codimension... (for codim-2 strata; higher codim strata don't have canonical orientations from ω alone — actually the condition is: for |I| = 2, D_{ij} symplectic and orientation agreement; then inductively higher intersections follow? In Li–Wu's definition, SC symplectic divisor requires all D_I symplectic and for each pair, orientation agreement. Actually the definition quoted in search result: "A simple crossings (or SC) symplectic divisor in (X,ω) is a finite transverse union V = ∪ V_i of closed submanifolds of codim 2 such that V_I is a symplectic submanifold for every I ⊂ S and the intersection and ω-orientations of V_I agree." OK so all strata symplectic with orientation agreement.)

Definition (ω-regularization). For a transverse collection {V_i}, an ω-regularization is a collection (R_i)_{i∈S} of "normal crossing data": each R_i consists of a Hermitian metric on NX V_i (i.e., metric + fiberwise complex structure i_i compatible... hmm) — precisely: an ω-compatible normalization of the normal bundle: a fiberwise complex structure i_i: NX V_i → NX V_i and a Hermitian metric, plus connection ∇^{(i)} on NX V_i satisfying: the ∇^{(i)}-parallel transport preserves i_i and the Hermitian metric; and for each I, the normal bundle NX V_I = ⊕_{i∈I} NX V_i ⊗ ... hmm; plus compatibility with the symplectic splitting of TX|_{V_I} via the intersection structure: the direct sum decomposition TX|_{V_I} = (⊕_{i∈I} NX V_i|_{V_I}) ⊕ T V_I is ω-compatible, connections preserve the splitting (tangent connection is the ω-LC connection... etc.).

Rather than reproduce the full definition (it's long — Definition 3.3/3.5 in 2112.13125, or Definition in 1410.0609), I'll state a schematic version and cite. For the proof I need the key consequences:

(a) The space Symp^+(X,D) of positive symplectic forms (making D an SC symplectic divisor) is nonempty (contains ω).
(b) Theorem (weak homotopy equivalences): Aux(X,D) → Symp^+(X,D) and AK(X,D) → Symp^+(X,D) are weak homotopy equivalences; in particular, there is a path ω_t of cohomologous forms in Symp^+(X,D), t∈[0,1], ω_0 = ω, such that (X,D,ω_1) admits a regularization R, and there exists J with (ω_1, R, J) ∈ AK(X,D). Also paths lift, so Aux fiber over ω_1 nonempty etc. Actually simplest: since Aux(X,D) → Symp^+(X,D) is a wHE and ω ∈ Symp^+, there exists (ω_1, R) ∈ Aux with a path from ω to ω_1 in Symp^+(X,D); since cohomologous-ness: the survey states the path can be taken cohomologous. Then AK(X,D) → Aux(X,D) wHE gives J with (ω_1, R, J) ∈ AK(X,D), i.e., J is ω_1-tame? Need: J ∈ J^ω(X) — the space of ω-tame almost complex structures, per the survey: "J ∈ J^ω(X)" such that V_I J-holomorphic, etc. Yes: AK(X,V) = space of triples (ω, R, J) where ω ∈ Symp^+, R an ω-regularization, and J ∈ J^ω(X) (tame) with compatibility conditions.

(c) Compatibility of J with R implies: for each I ⊂ S, J preserves TV_I ⊂ TX|_{V_I} and Ψ_I intertwines J with J_{R;I} on the normal bundle. In particular, each D_i and each stratum D_I is J-holomorphic, and near D_I, in the "normal-crossing coordinates" provided by the regularization, J is a product: J = J|_{D_I} ⊕ i on (T D_I ⊕ NX D_I).

**Lemma 3.2(a) detailed statement/proof:** Let B be a manifold with almost complex structure J_B; let Ñ_i (i ∈ I) be complex rank-1 real-2 bundles over B with fiberwise complex structures i_i; ∇ a connection on Ñ := ⊕Ñ_i preserving each Ñ_i and each i_i. On the total space E of Ñ, define J̃ := the split structure: T_{(x,v)}E = H_{(x,v)} ⊕ V_{(x,v)} (∇-splitting), J̃ := (J_B-lift) ⊕ (⊕i_i). Let W ⊆ B open, σ ∈ Γ(W, Ñ_i^*) nonvanishing with ∂̄^∇σ = 0 (i.e., the (0,1)-part of ∇^*σ vanishes, ∇^* the dual connection). Define w: π^{-1}(W) → ℂ, w(x, v) := σ(x)(v_i). Then: (i) w is J̃-holomorphic; (ii) {w = 0} = π^{-1}({v_i = 0}-region...) hmm: {w = 0} = {(x,v): σ(x)(v_i) = 0} = {v_i = 0} ✓ (σ ≠ 0); (iii) dw ≠ 0 along {w = 0}: dw at (x, 0)... at points with v_i = 0: the vertical derivative: d_vw(η) = σ(η_i) ≠ 0 for η_i ≠ 0 ✓ so dw ≠ 0 on the hypersurface {v_i = 0} except...: need dw ≠ 0 AT EVERY point of {v_i = 0}: vertical directions Ñ_i: d_vw|_{Ñ_i-vertical} = σ(x)(·) ≠ 0 ✓✓. 
Proof of (i): Let X ∈ T_{(x,v)}E: write X = ξ^H + η^V with ξ ∈ T_xB, η ∈ Ñ_x. Then J̃X = (J_Bξ)^H + (iη)^V. Compute: dw(X) = d w(ξ^H) + dw(η^V): dw(η^V) = σ(x)(η_i) (vertical derivative: w is linear in v_i on fibers ✓). dw(ξ^H): derivative along the horizontal curve: w(x(t), v(t)) with v ∇-parallel: d/dt σ(x(t))(v_i(t)) = (∇^*_ξσ)(v_i) + σ(∇_ξv_i = 0) = (∇^*ξσ)(v_i): so dw(ξ^H) = (∇^*σ(v_i-part... formally: ⟨∇^*σ, v_i⟩(ξ)). Then dw(J̃X) = ⟨∇^*σ, v_i⟩(J_Bξ) + σ(i_iη_i): and i·dw(X) = i⟨∇^*σ, v_i⟩(ξ) + iσ(η_i). Now σ(i_iη_i) = iσ(η_i) ✓ (σ complex-linear on the fiber ✓). And ⟨∇^*σ, v_i⟩(J_Bξ) − i⟨∇^*σ, v_i⟩(ξ) = ⟨∇^*σ, v_i⟩^{(0,1)}-part-conditions: = ⟨(∇^*σ)^{(0,1)}, v_i⟩(J_Bξ)·... : precisely: write the ℂ-valued 1-form β := ⟨∇^*σ, v_i⟩ ∈ Ω¹(W; ℂ): condition: β∘J_B = iβ ⟺ β^{(0,1)} = 0: and β^{(0,1)} = ⟨(∇^*σ)^{(0,1)}, v_i⟩ = ⟨∂̄^∇σ, v_i⟩ = 0 ✓ by hypothesis ✓✓. Hence dw∘J̃ = i·dw ✓. ∎ Clean ✓.

**Deep Structure Theorem (DST) statement:** Let (X, ω) be a closed symplectic manifold and V = ∪V_i an SC symplectic divisor. Then: (1) There is a continuous family (ω_t)_{t∈[0,1]} of symplectic forms on X with [ω_t] = [ω] for all t, such that V is an SC symplectic divisor with respect to every ω_t, and such that V admits an ω_1-regularization; (2) with R an ω_1-regularization, there exist ω_1-tame (indeed the space is nonempty and weakly contractible-type) almost complex structures J with (ω_1, R, J) ∈ AK; (3) an ω_1-regularization determines, along every stratum V_I, an ω_1-orthogonal splitting TX|_{V_I} = TV_I ⊕ ⊕_{i∈I}Ñ_i with fiberwise complex structures i_i satisfying ω_1(u, i_iu) > 0, coherent under inclusions of strata; and the same holds for the induced SC configurations on all strata with the restricted forms. [Citations: CRAS Thm 7 + text; 1410.0609 Thm 2.12 + normalization; 2112.13125 Defs 3.3, 3.5, §2.]

**Proposition (Auxiliary divisors; cite Donaldson):** Let (B, ω_B) closed symplectic and 𝒞 a finite collection of symplectic submanifolds; c ∈ H²(B;ℤ). Then for all sufficiently large k there exist symplectic divisors Z⁺, Z⁻ ⊂ B with [Z⁺] = c + k[ω_B]', [Z⁻] = k[ω_B]' (integral classes approximating), such that: {Z⁺, Z⁻} ∪ 𝒞 (as a transverse collection) is an SC symplectic configuration in the sense of Definition 2.1 (transverse; all strata symplectic; orientation conditions), and c₁-compatibility: the complex line bundle with c₁ = c is isomorphic to O(Z⁺)⊗O(Z⁻)^*.
